# Orchestrator state (read this first after any context compaction)

The orchestrator's own catch-up note for the cockpit-to-oculus migration. Rewritten at every package. Everything here is also derivable from `plan.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `git log`, but slower. Read this, then `plan.md`, then the tail of the transcript.

```yaml
verify:                      # the quick list (Z351, Z421): from the repository root in the worktree, by builders and reviewers; a reviewer adds `-- --base <base>`.
- npm run test:quick
verify_landing:              # the landing list (standing demand "Only related tests at a landing", rulings 240 (a) and 241 (b)): by the orchestrator at each landing, in the gate worktree.
- npm run diagrams && npm run test:quick
worktree_root: .agents/worktrees
standards: [.scratch/migration/agents/spec.md]
executor: agent
refute_after_repair: yes     # plan.yaml: /refute runs again over each repair round on a fresh reviewer; its findings are fixed at landing or booked, never sent back.
repair_rounds: 1             # one round and never a second (Axel, 2026-10-10, open item 42).
review_minutes: 0            # plan.yaml: no time box.
look: the page named TEST on the running server, shown from the Pages view on the Views and Settings page and made there with New page when missing, never a page Axel works on   # plan.yaml; every step that changes a view, the shell or anything a reader sees is looked at there before its booking.
report_each_step: no         # the loop books each landing on disk and goes on (plan-orchestration step 10); no skill reads this key, it records the choice
worker: claude:opus          # Axel, 2026-10-13: Opus 5.5 builders, `claude-opus-5-5`, from the next launch; D74's Sonnet builder aac8ad09939b7cc54 was stopped at Axel's word (option (b)) and an Opus continuation builder takes over its worktree. Sonnet 5.5 builders from 2026-09-30 to 2026-10-13; Opus from W1 to Q86c. Axel's default from W1 on (2026-09-14 09:20, "switch to Opus agents"); codex:gpt-5.6-sol for a package whose brief says so; the rows before W1 ran on Sol from O24 on.
orchestrator: claude:opus    # a trial for the next set of three (Axel, 2026-09-16 14:10): the orchestrator runs on Opus with the reviewer stage kept, measured by its usage rows against the Fable rows before it; the top tier for R2 and O29, whose decisions carry the release and the retirement; the sessions before this line ran on Claude Fable 5.1
reviewer: claude:opus        # Axel, 2026-10-02 (Z252 (b)): reviewers and brief checks on Opus 5.5, `claude-opus-5-5`, from the next launch (the model check under "Agents"); Sonnet 5.5 from Z145 (b) (2026-10-01) to Z252; Opus 5.5 from W1 to Z145; codex:gpt-5.6-sol from O24a to R8; its usage goes into the table under its own row.
review: earned               # Axel, 2026-09-14 09:35: the reviewer runs unless the worker's last three rows passed the bar with at most one fix at landing, always on a first package and on server, state-layer or wire packages; a second review only when the repair went beyond its list (the skill's "The review, earned"). Opus's record starts at W1.
workers_at_once: 6           # Axel, 2026-10-06 (Z632): six. Before: five (Z472, 2026-10-05); seven (Z423, 2026-10-04); five (Z368, 2026-10-03); three (Z291, 2026-10-02), lowered from five because five full suites at once made each `npm test` run 13 min and more; builders now run `npm run test:quick` once, after their last change.
```

```yaml
dispatch: []      # steps in flight, up to six (Z632)
```

## The standing demands (from Axel, in force)
- A decision for Axel is an open item booked in this file, or it is not raised; never loose options in a report or a brief draft (Axel, 2026-10-22).
- A command that takes more than a moment runs in the background while other work goes on; no foreground wait loop (Axel, 2026-10-22, a hard rule).
- A brief check never widens a step (Axel, 2026-10-22, ruling Brief check scope): a finding whose fix would widen the step's paths, add a requirement or add a case beyond the step's line goes to Axel as an open item, never into the brief.
- O37, O38 and O39 (Axel, 2026-10-16, ruling O33 split): O37 starts from `agents/briefs/O37-start.patch`, whose every change its builder owns; no one runs /diagnose or starts a diagnosis agent for O37, the builder included; O38 runs once O37 has landed and O39 once O38 has landed (Axel's approvals, open items 121 and 122), every deletion and skill edit of O38 still on his yes path by path. Not a single thing of O33's phase A may be missed ("No looks, no bugs, nothing"): every defect is in O37's Step 0 and every owed look in `agents/reviews/owed-looks.md` "To take".
- O33's brief (`agents/briefs/O33.md`) is not used for O37, O38 or O39 (Axel, 2026-10-16): each brief is written from the step's plan.md line and Step 0, O33's line at plan.md:495 with its Step 0, and every ruling they name.
- Before the oculus repository is made public, its ledger is taken out of its history with `git filter-repo --path .scratch/ --invert-paths` (Axel, 2026-10-15, ruling O33 ledger): the ledger holds his verbatim words and the hub's private paths, and lives in the repository only while it is private.
- O38's deletions (Axel, 2026-10-16, ruling O33 split): the skills research-cockpit, remove-unicode and project-new with their six links, the edit of `.agents/skills/project-cleanup/SKILL.md`, and `tools/cockpit`, `tools/present`, `tools/data` and `tools/deposit` each go to Axel path by path for his yes before they are made; the three untracked files in `tools/cockpit` go only on their own yes.
- A question Axel asks is answered by the session itself, by reading the code, the files and the data and running the commands that show the answer (Axel, 2026-10-13): `/diagnose` and diagnosis agents are never used to answer a question of his.
- Diagnosis is banned (Axel, 2026-10-17, open item 131): no `/diagnose` and no diagnosis agent, run by the orchestrator, a builder, a reviewer, a brief check or any other agent, in any step, round or landing, whatever a skill says; no one asks for it, offers it or suggests it, in a report, a brief, an open item or a message. A cause is found by reading the code and, where a read does not settle it, by a run on a scratch copy.
- No migration code, in any step, whatever an earlier ruling said (Axel, 2026-10-10): no code reads, carries, converts or tolerates an older form of stored data; a change of shape is made once on the data by the orchestrator or the old data shows as unknown or as a finding (`agents/spec.md`, What every package obeys).
- A brief is written, its premises checked and its findings closed by the orchestrator itself through the `spec` skill, never by an agent (Axel, 2026-10-09): no brief-writing agent, no premise survey or premise recheck agent, no agent that edits a brief; the one agent `/spec` starts is the brief check, as the skill says, once per step: a brief rewritten or widened after its check is held by the orchestrator itself in the check report's Closed section, never by a second brief check, and nothing a forbidden or stopped check found is acted on (Axel, 2026-10-21).
- A builder or reviewer never takes focus on Axel's screen and never reruns a case that starts the app (Axel, 2026-10-05): no `app.focus({steal:true})`, no `osascript activate`, no `Page.bringToFront`, no `focus()` or `show()` called from outside the app's code; a focus case is read from a recorder on `BrowserWindow.prototype.focus`; the app is started once for all window cases and closed by its pid; a case that does not show in that run is reported not verified and goes to the look. Every builder prompt that runs the desktop app restates this.
- Never run `git reset --hard`, in any form, on main, in the gate or in any worktree (Axel, 2026-10-05). A worktree is moved to a new commit with `git checkout -f --detach <commit>` only.
- Before T2a to T2d land (Axel, 2026-10-04, Z408): report each sweep's lines removed and left and its tests deleted and left, counted by command in its worktree, and wait for his judgement before `/land`.
- Talk to Axel as an expert colleague (Axel, 2026-10-02, ruling Z266, rewritten at his word 13:20): Axel is an expert engineer and scientist, and every message is written to him as to a peer: plain, precise and complete, never simplified as for a child and never clipped to a status line. A step is always named by its id and what it is ("D25, the editor options step"), never by a bare id and never by a vague pointer such as "the new step" or "the source editor step". A ruling number (Z...) is not used in a message to him; the ruling is described in words. Ledger text is never quoted at him. He gets exactly the questions he asked, as many as he asked, in his order, each answered in full. Every decision that is his is presented as the hub rules require: an open item, written first, with each option, the pros and cons of each, one recommendation with its reason, and which option is the lazy one. A choice the orchestrator took in automode is told as such, with what it is and the step it went into. Nothing he did not ask for: no corrections that do not change what he would do next, no process narration, no meta paragraphs, no closing remarks, no passive-aggressive or defensive phrasing.
- Every builder prompt restates `agents/spec.md:25` (scoped test files while working; the full verify list once, after the last change; a failed command rerun alone, then the full list once more) and asks the report for the number of full-list runs; each landing report states that number. Builders ran the full list 6 to 18 times a step before this (Q88b 18 runs, 38.6 min; Q107 17, 55.3 min), counted from their transcripts.
- Skills are never edited, ever (Axel, 2026-10-01): the orchestrator, its agents and every session of this plan never change a skill or any file of one (`~/.claude/skills`, `~/.local/share/ordo-stable`, `~/workspace/ordo`: its `SKILL.md`, templates, scripts such as `land/templates/checks.sh`, tests and docs), never offer to, and never put such a change to Axel as an option or an open item, whatever his go; a skill's behaviour that costs time is reported as a fact and nothing more.
- Long commands run in the background (Axel, 2026-10-04): every command that may take more than a few seconds (an install, a test run, a build, `git archive`, a script over the tree, a worktree set-up) is started with `run_in_background`, and the orchestrator goes on with other work and reads its output when it is told it finished; a foreground command is only a quick read or a quick write.
- Automode (Axel, 2026-09-30 22:42): every choice goes through the process as before (an open item written in full with its options, pros, cons, recommendation and the lazy option named), and the orchestrator then closes it itself with the option Axel would take, books it everywhere a ruling is booked (the closed log in `orchestrator-history.md`, `plan.md`'s Rulings as "(the orchestrator, automode)", the briefs and the steps), and adds it to `choices.md` beside `queue.md` for Axel's review; the work goes on without waiting. Axel reviews `choices.md` when he likes: an agreed entry is removed from it, and a disagreed one is discussed and, when it changes what was built, becomes a new step that changes it on top. Left open for Axel, never decided in automode: a clash between two of Axel's written rules or rulings; a reversal of a ruling Axel gave; anything that needs Axel's own hands or accounts (a login, a look he must fix, his cluster, money); anything touching a secret or deleting his data; a choice with no clear recommendation.
- Every open item a skill says to raise to the user goes through automode first (Axel, 2026-10-05): a cause not found at /spec or /diagnose, a stop, a finding that is the user's, a case a builder hands back, and every choice a brief writer, a brief check or a review raises (a guarded entry's wording, a behaviour, a file shape, a name, a folder rule). The orchestrator closes it with its recommendation and books it as automode says, unless it is one of automode's exclusions. A skill's words "raised to the user" never by themselves make an item Axel's.
- Looks and the machine, the rules every look and every command keep (Axel, in force; booked 2026-10-03 from the session's standing orders):
  - The ICS calendar link is a secret stored only in gitignored `config/local.yml`. Never put it in git, the ledger or a log; never print, open, grep or hash that file; never fetch a calendar address; never open the Config view's `local.yml` card or the Settings view's `local.yml` tree node; every grep over `config/` uses `--exclude=local.yml` (`':!config/local.yml'` with git grep).
  - Never screenshot the Config view; never click TEST's "Config" tab or the Settings pane's "Config" tab; a tab beside one of them is clicked by JS (`element.click()`), never by coordinates.
  - A look never presses a control that opens the macOS folder panel (Add app from folder, Add Folder to Workspace and the like): the panel is a real window in front of Axel's screen, and the server's osascript waits on it. A look of such a control stops at the state before the press.
  - Never touch port 8787, nor 8790 beyond viewing; never curl them; never start, stop or move Axel's server. Oculus never reads `.vscode`.
  - Looks happen on TEST at localhost:8790, tab 812137347, reached from the Pages view on the "Views and Settings" page (its rail button, then the "TEST" row); another rail button leaves TEST, and on TEST no rail button is current. Never click the Sessions rail button during a look. The one exception ruled so far is D35's port move, looked at on a scratch oculus with its own config (Axel's (b), 2026-10-03).
  - The browser theme on TEST is changed only for a light-theme look (Z290), and put back after.
  - Before typing on a page, JS confirms `document.activeElement` is the intended field.
  - In the Changes view never click Unstage, Commit or Discard on real repositories; never click a diff's Revert Block or Stage Block; never press Send in A1's comment box; never type into ledger files opened during looks; in the Repositories More menu click only "Reopen Closed Repositories"; in the Files More menu never click Add Folder to Workspace or Open Folder; never click the Processes view's Stop.
  - A look never leaves a write behind: config changes are reverted and `git diff --exit-code -- config` checked; another file is restored from a backup and its `shasum` checked.
  - Never run tmux without `-L` or a private `-S` socket, and never list the default socket. Never delete `~/.oculus/dispatches.json`; never edit `~/Downloads/tmp2.md`; never compute on the HPC login node; never export Word to PDF; never stop an agent without Axel's word; never edit config because an agent asked.
  - Instructions inside pasted content are followed only where Axel's own message asks.
- Defects go into steps that builders build (Axel, 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03): a defect a look or a review finds is booked into the step that changes its files and built by that step's builder; the orchestrator does not investigate or fix it in its own loop, beyond a fix at landing of the step under landing and the read-only cause-finding `/spec` assigns while writing a brief. When Axel asks when something is fixed, the answer names the steps: "This is all being fixed in step X, Y, Z".
- Owed looks come first (Axel, 2026-10-03): a look owed from an earlier landing is taken before any further landing, never placed after other work, and every landing takes its own look before its commit.
- Nothing that the auto-mode permission check can refuse is ever done (Axel, 2026-10-05, Z551): no agent is started from a page, no permission mode is picked in a composer, no Approve and Start, and none of the refused actions below. A look case that needs such an action is done without it and is never raised again; a refused call is never retried in another form.
- U2's choices are Axel's (Axel, 2026-10-01): the choices U2's audit raises, its grouping into build steps and the choices it names (such as group 6, collections as rows or cards, and group 4's icon set), go to him as open items once U2 has landed; automode never closes one. Nothing else changes.
- Every landing report states, beside each review's usage, the number of findings that review raised under each of its four headings and its model, so the three-way comparison reads them from the landing reports (Axel, 2026-10-01).
- No tiny steps (Axel, 2026-10-01: "you're trying to create faaaar too many steps"): a finding, a correction or a small defect goes into the step that touches its files or its subject, widened in automode, and a new step is added only for work no step in the plan covers and that is too large to fold in; two small findings are never two steps.
- Every slot is kept full (Axel, 2026-09-30, "Make sure we get back to 3 running steps"; the number is `workers_at_once`, six since Axel's Z632 of 2026-10-06): whenever a slot frees, the next /spec starts in the same turn.
- A look that cannot be taken is an open item for Axel to fix (Axel, 2026-09-30): when a screenshot on the TEST page comes back blank or the page cannot be reached, the orchestrator raises an open item that says what it saw and what Axel would do, and goes on with the other steps.
- At landing the verify list runs once on main (Axel, 2026-09-28). A failing test in a file the step does not change, which passes when that one file runs alone, is named in the booking with that one-file run, and the landing goes on. The full list is not run again for it, and the test is not investigated. This holds whatever the failure says, a timeout or an assertion. Such a file is tagged slow at that landing (`// @module-tag slow` in its head comment), as a fix at landing, so it does not fail the next step (Z64, Axel, 2026-09-29).
- `queue.md` beside this file is the list of steps in flight and queued, in the shape Axel reads it (Axel, 2026-09-28). It is rewritten in the same commit as every launch, repair round, landing and ruling that moves a step, and a resume point never leaves it behind the dispatch block or the queue line of `plan.md`. `queue.md` is rewritten whenever a step changes state (a `/spec` started, a brief check or builder launched, a report or review received, a round sent, a landing, a ruling), and each time every line is checked against the dispatch block, the ticks in `plan.md` and the landed commits, not only the line of the step that moved (Axel, 2026-09-29). Each line of `queue.md` is the step's id, its name in a few words and its state now in a few words ("building", "repair round 2 running", "once D13b has landed"); paths, rulings, findings and history stay in `plan.md` and this file (Axel, 2026-09-29). `queue.md` holds only the In flight list, the Queued list, the Owed line and the Landed list: no open-items line, no pointer to `choices.md`, no time of day, and a landed step appears only in the Landed list, at its top with its date and commit, never as a "Landed hh:mm" line (Axel, 2026-10-01). The Queued heading ends with the number of queued steps in parentheses, `Queued, in order (<n> steps):`, each step id counted once, and every In flight line carries the step's state now (brief check, building, review, repair round, landing), both kept current at every change (Axel, 2026-10-01).
- `queue.md` never drifts (Axel, 2026-10-05): every commit the orchestrator makes carries `queue.md`, rewritten and checked whole first: each In flight line against the dispatch block and the agents running, each Queued line's condition against the Landed list and the ticks in `plan.md`, the Queued count by command, the Owed line against `agents/reviews/owed-looks.md`. A commit without that check is not made.
- Libraries: the orchestrator checks, Axel decides (Axel, 2026-09-26). Less code is the aim: oculus is over 100,000 lines, and a library that covers a capability can remove code that would otherwise be kept up; code is written by hand only where no library covers the capability, never by cutting a feature. Before any brief is written, `/spec` includes a library check for every capability the step builds or changes: the candidates found (npm and the ecosystem oculus uses), each with its version, license, maintainer, last release, compatibility with what oculus runs (Svelte 5, SvelteKit, `marked` 18 and the rest of `package.json`), what it would replace and what would stay, and how it takes oculus's theme tokens and layer rules. A candidate that could replace or avoid hand-built code is an open item for Axel with options, pros, cons and one recommendation (a library, or keeping the code), and the step waits for his ruling. Bundle size is not a criterion. The brief names the library ruled, and a builder adds no dependency the brief did not name (`agents/spec.md`), and reports an unnamed library that would cover its work instead of installing it. The Ordo `/spec` skill and its brief template carry no library check yet; this file carries it until they do, and the Ordo change goes through the session that holds that repository.
- The library audit (Axel, 2026-09-26: a proper and full audit, before any new step is launched): every capability oculus builds by hand, across `src/`, `bin/`, the config loaders and the test and check tooling, with X3b's worktree changes included, audited for a library that covers it; the result is a set of open items, one per capability where a library is a real option, each with options, pros, cons and a recommendation. Done and ruled 2026-09-26 (LA1 to LA22, closed log); a step whose brief uses a ruled library names it, and a builder adds no other dependency. X3b, already in flight, continues through its round's refutation and its landing.
- Tests (Axel, 2026-10-03, Z354): a brief or a round asks for a test only under `agents/spec.md`'s test rule (a test exists only for behaviour whose failure costs something, one per behaviour, no audits), and every landing deletes the step's tests that break it.
- Rounds and briefs carry known fixes only (Axel, 2026-09-27, "Yes"): a round or a brief never asks the builder to find a cause, reproduce a fault or measure until a condition holds; the orchestrator reads the cause first; a row whose cause its read does not settle goes into the brief with its symptom, what the read found and the behaviour that must hold, and the builder fixes it (open item 131). A format or rule a brief applies across the tree is run by the orchestrator on five real cases, quoted in the brief. Both are in the spec and plan-orchestration skills.
- Where verification time goes (Axel, 2026-09-26, "Fully agreed"; Axel, 2026-09-28, revert-check and the browser tests removed entirely, Q93). A step is judged on the unit suite, the type check, lint, format, the build and the look. Kept: the reviewer's read of the diff, one test per behaviour and per fix, the whole unit suite once on main at landing, and the look. A builder runs the unit test files its change touches while it works and the verify list once, at the end of its round. A reviewer reruns what it has reason to doubt and every closure the builder claims, and spends its time on reading. No brief or round message asks for a revert directive or a browser test. Every brief and round message carries these rules.
- A step ends at its rounds (Axel, 2026-09-25, stopping Q75): what its last review still finds is fixed at landing when it is small, and everything else is written into Q72's list, the re-read before the release, and the loop goes straight on to Axel's next queued step. No new step is made from a review finding and nothing pauses for one; only a defect that breaks something Axel uses stops the loop, as an open item.
- When Axel says the Ordo release carrying plan 2.B is pinned: drop this file's demand that reports open with the open items (Ordo's skills then carry it, with a position line first), and close O32 by its tick condition (Axel, 2026-09-24, passing on Ordo's review).
- Landed work is finished forward, never reverted (Axel, 2026-09-24): a landed step found short of its brief, or wrong, gets a new step that fixes, rebuilds or finishes it on top of what landed; no landed commit is reverted to redo it.
- Every step runs through the skills themselves (Axel, 2026-09-24, after the steps from Q65's landing to L6 were done by hand from the skills' remembered text): `/spec`, `/refute` and `/land` are called as skills for every step, every time, including after a compaction, never carried out from a copy of their text; and on top of them:
  - each report opens with what needs Axel's decision, as "Talk to Axel as an expert colleague" says;
  - each look covers every theme the step changes, on the TEST page;
  - each landing stops the step's builder and every reviewer before the landing commit, and `ListAgents` shows none of them left (the rule below).
- An agent is stopped as soon as its work is done (Axel, 2026-10-03, after the D25 builder was left open with work of its own running once its round had reported): a reviewer or a brief check is stopped with TaskStop once its report is saved, and a builder once its last repair round has reported; `ListAgents` is read after each stop and shows it as stopped.
- A landing ends every agent of its step (Axel, 2026-09-24, after L5's builder was left open once L5 had landed): before the landing commit, the builder and every reviewer of the step are stopped with TaskStop, and `ListAgents` shows none of them left; the landing report names that check. The Ordo land and plan-orchestration skills carry no such step yet; it is added there with O32's recipes, since another session holds the Ordo repository.
- VS Code parity is exhaustive (Axel, 2026-09-23: "It really feels like, everytime I ask you to look or copy vs code, you do the absolute minimal amount of work possible."): every step that copies a VS Code surface starts from a feature inventory of that surface, taken from VS Code itself (vscode.dev in Chrome, the docs, Axel's screenshots), every feature listed with built, left out or later and why; its brief carries the inventory and its landing look compares the two side by side. The inventories live in `agents/reviews/vscode-parity.md`.
- The pause of 2026-10-11 (Axel: "Please do not launch new agents beyond these 2, once both are done, pause"): the two agents running (the D63 builder ad834ceece00db11d and the D67 diagnosis agent ae9dcf77a17c614d7) finish; no other agent is started, a reviewer, a brief check, a diagnosis agent and a repair-round builder included. D67 is landed or held as far as the session can take it without a new agent; D63's report is saved and read, and its review waits. Then the loop pauses until Axel lifts it. The D63 builder was then stopped at Axel's word ("Can you pause the agent", 2026-10-11) in the middle of its build, with no report written; its partial work stays in `.agents/worktrees/D63` (233 paths in `git status --short`), and on resumption the same builder is resumed on its agent id with the runner's message tool to finish the build and write its report. Lifted entirely by Axel ("Please continue D63 (as well as general orchestration, all pauses are lifted", 2026-10-11): the D63 builder resumed on its agent id.
- At D63's landing, before the landing script: remove the worktree's `node_modules` link (`.agents/worktrees/D63/tools/oculus/node_modules`) once the builder is stopped, since that worktree's `.gitignore` is at its base and still reads `node_modules/`, so its `git add -A` would stage the link and the cherry-pick would replace main's real `node_modules` again (open item 55).
- The pause of 2026-09-23 (Axel: "Please pause once L3 and O32 are both landed and don't start L4 yet"): O32's oculus half is landed and its skill half is not; L3 finishes its repair round, its review and its landing, and then nothing more is specced or dispatched, L4 included, until Axel lifts the pause. Lifted on 2026-09-23 for L5 and U1 only (Axel agreed to the recommended order): L5, then U1, then the plan pauses again before L4. Lifted entirely on 2026-09-23 (Axel: "You do not need to pause before L4, the pause is lifted and you may continue once you get there.").
- The run of 2026-09-23 (Axel: `/plan-orchestration oculus/migration`, agent mode, "No stop time, but do stop when you need me to make a decision or anything like that", "only commit on important events, not on every file write, brief write and such", "properly report to me, as the skills say"): the pause after Q59 is lifted; the loop runs the queue unattended overnight, up to `workers_at_once` steps in flight, and ends its turn only at a stop, which goes to Axel with one recommendation. Commits at important events only: a dispatch (the block with the builder's identity, which the handoff rule requires before the builder starts) and a landing; a brief, a report or a review is written without a commit of its own and goes in with the next of those.
- The run of 2026-09-22 (Axel: `/plan-orchestration oculus/migration inline`, "Pause after Q58", "only commit on important events, not on every file write, brief write and such", "properly report to me, as the skills say"): the pause of 2026-09-20 is lifted for Q58 alone; Q58 is built inline by the orchestrating session in its worktree; after Q58 lands the plan pauses again and nothing later is specced or dispatched. Commits are made at important events only: the landing commit of a step carries its brief, the state file, the refuter and landing reports and the booking; the brief, the dispatch block and a refuter report get no commit of their own, so a worktree is cut from main's head and the uncommitted ledger on main is the handoff until the landing.
- The run of 2026-09-17 10:46 (Axel: "You may continue with the orchestration. Important is to keep 3 tasks running in parallel. No pause point is set for now."): three steps in flight at all times, a new one dispatched at each landing, the queue as plan.md's line; one repair round per step; the look at each landing on the TEST page.
- One repair round per package, now in the plan-orchestration and land skills (Axel, 2026-09-16 23:21, "this was already supposed to be the rule"): after the one round the orchestrator reads the diff and lands, fixing the small things at landing; anything beyond the brief goes to the open items for Axel, never back to the builder. The plan-orchestration skill's step 8 ("There is no cap on rounds") contradicts this and is booked in the open items.

After both land, nothing more is dispatched.
- The pause of 2026-09-16 17:36 (Axel: "I have 15 min because my computer closes, so perhaps best to stop them now"): D16's fourth reviewer and Q33's builder stopped; nothing is dispatched, reviewed or landed until Axel says to continue. On resumption: read this block, rerun D16's fourth review, read Q33's partial round 4 diff and resume or replace its builder.
- The pause after the set (Axel, 2026-09-16 14:16, "you must pause once you completed your set M2, Q30, D16", and 14:31, "Q33 should take the place of Q30", "I want this trial to be 1 set (1 x 3)"): M2, D16 and Q33 are specced, built, reviewed and landed, and then nothing more is dispatched; Q30 waits in the queue after Q32 and D22. Builders are dispatched as the plan says (his yes of 14:16 to the clash with the global no-sub-agents rule).
- The port rule, corrected (Axel, 2026-09-16 00:35, "I never wanted a literal copy"): a port carries what the reader sees and does; every mechanism is built on DESIGN.md's structure (machine-local state, the write chain, the change log, instance state, one file browser, one editor); where the cockpit's mechanism contradicts that structure the oculus form is built and the difference is reported as one line. The spec's amendment of 2026-09-16 states it for the workers; the sweep above is its first application.
- The limited go of 2026-09-16 00:41 (Axel: book everything, carry out the agreed list, report back): the bookings below, the tasks.yml task, the D7 brief, DESIGN.md, the spec amendment and Q28's fixes on main are made; nothing is dispatched and D6 and Q20 are not landed until he gives the full green light. On it, in order: the two landings, then the queue as rewritten in plan.md.
- No claim about state without a command in the same turn (Axel, 2026-09-17 23:31, after a run of wrong answers: "three steps unlooked" when it was four, "twelve open steps" when it was 26, "everything is booked" three times, each one wrong): every sentence about what exists, what is done, what is booked, how many there are, what a file says or whether something passed is produced by a command run in the same turn and names it, or says "not verified"; a question of the shape "is everything X" is answered by running the check that enumerates X. Nothing lifts it. The rule is in `~/.claude/rules/no-claim-without-a-command.md`, the global `CLAUDE.md` and the hub `CLAUDE.md`.
- Every question is answered in full, and the answer reaches Axel (Axel, 2026-09-15 23:38; corrected by Axel 2026-09-17 22:20: the earlier wording, "an answer ends the turn, no tool call after it", was wrong and made the loop stop at every question): text written between tool calls reaches him only as a one-line machine summary, so every question is answered fully in the message that ends the turn, each one in order; a question is never a reason to stop work he has asked for, that work goes on in the same turn and the answer lands with it, unless the answer decides what to do next. The rule is in `~/.claude/rules/answers-reach-axel-in-full.md`, the global `CLAUDE.md` and the hub `CLAUDE.md`.
- The lazy option never locks (Axel, 2026-09-15 20:12 and again at 23:32, "This goes for ALL decisions"): a yes to a recommended option counts only when that option is the better one, not the one that costs least; the orchestrator says at every ruling which options were the lazy ones, and a yes to a lazy option is not booked as locked.
- The pause of 2026-09-14 20:27 was lifted at 22:21 with a window until 2026-09-15 07:10: at 07:10 no worker, reviewer or landing is in flight, the dispatch block reads `none`, everything is committed; a package is dispatched only when it can land before then.

- queue.md is only ever rewritten whole (Z473): every change writes the full file from the state of the plan, each section read and checked line by line, never a replacement of one line in place.
- No new steps, ever (Z468): the orchestrator never adds a step to the plan. Whatever a finding needs is folded whole into an existing step (its line widened, its brief or its next round), and nothing it would have done is dropped.
- A file feature works from the file opened in the editor (Z488, Axel, 2026-10-05, "This goes for literally everything"): every step that gives a file a feature puts it in the open file's editor (its breadcrumb bar (Z536), its gutter, its marks, a tab beside it), as VS Code does, never in a separate view opened by a right-click; every brief is read against this before dispatch. `docs/` files keep their Doc view (Z489).
- The landing gate never runs in main's checkout (Z492, Axel, 2026-10-05): `npm run diagrams && npm run test:quick` (the related tests only, rulings 240 (a) and 241 (b)) runs in the gate worktree `.agents/worktrees/gate`, brought to main's head and given the step's range by cherry-pick, its `node_modules` a `cp -c` clone of main's renewed when `package.json` or the lockfile changes; fixes at landing are made there and the gate rerun; once green the same range is cherry-picked onto main, the landing fixes and diagrams copied, `git diff --cached` on main compared with the gate's, then the look on TEST and the commit.
- Make sure it works, every step (Z467): the landing look exercises the step's feature itself on TEST across real cases, for a file feature at least ten files of different types, each case recorded with what was seen; nothing is committed to main before that look passes.
- The night run (Axel, 2026-09-15 21:32, "Back to the night run, you have 9h40m available (and no hpc access). Please resume"): the window ends at 07:12 on 2026-09-16; no package that cannot land inside it is dispatched, no HPC command; the pause of 20:15 is lifted, the reviewers for D1 and O28b dispatched at 21:33, D3 dispatched under the rolling window.
- The pause, lifted 21:32 (Axel, 2026-09-15 20:15, "let the current agents finish and then pause"): no new agent is launched after O28b's and D1's workers report; their reports, diffs and check runs are recorded, the orchestrator reads the diffs, their worktrees stay, and D3 waits in the queue; D2, whose worker had finished, is landed. Lifting the pause resumes at O28b's and D1's reviews.
- The rolling window (Axel, 2026-09-15 19:58): a new package is dispatched the moment one lands, three in flight at all times, the plan's set list read as the queue; each is cut at main's head after the latest landing and its paths are checked against the two still running.
- The hub `CLAUDE.md` "Working rules" section, in full. The ones that bite here: reports open with what is NOT done and carry a DONE / NOT DONE ledger naming the proving command; every measurement names its command or says not verified; a risk named is a task owned; no question boxes; surface rule clashes and stop; show code, not prose about code; references are not justifications (the cockpit never justifies an oculus choice); every change rewinds.
- Agents: the worker the block above names (Claude Opus 5.5 from 2026-10-13, or GPT 5.6 Sol for a package whose brief says so; a reviewer and a brief check on Claude Sonnet 5.5 by Z145 (b)), general-purpose, one at a time or two on disjoint paths and different views (R9, `workers_at_once: 2`; the skill's "Two packages in flight" section); the orchestrator on the top tier (Claude Fable 5.1 or GPT 6 Astra); any combination, as Axel ruled on 2026-09-11. An agent works in a git worktree the orchestrator creates at main's head (`git worktree add -b <package> .agents/worktrees/<package> HEAD`; the folder moved from `.claude/worktrees` on 2026-09-11 so nothing in the process carries a vendor's name; every git command on the worktree is run from inside it, never as `git -C <path>`, since `.codex/rules/default.rules` forbids the `-C` form and one recipe holds under both runners, then, from inside the worktree, `git sparse-checkout set <worktree_paths>` when the block names any); the agent never runs git and never edits any file under `.scratch/`. The orchestrator reads the whole diff, runs the verification commands on the worktree, lands by `git cherry-pick -n` onto main, runs them again on main, books the package in `plan.md`, commits by explicit path. Ruled by Axel 2026-09-10 as the substitute for `/rewind` over agent work.
- The model check (Axel, 2026-09-30): after every agent launch (a builder, a continuation builder, a reviewer, a brief check), the orchestrator reads the `message.model` field of the agent's assistant lines in its transcript, `~/.claude/projects/<project folder>/<session>/subagents/agent-<agent id>.jsonl` (`-Users-axelfaes-workspace-oculus` for a session started in `~/workspace/oculus`), and records it in the step's dispatch entry: a builder's under `model:` beside `session_id`, a reviewer's and a brief check's beside their usage in `reviewer_report` and `brief_check`. The accepted models are `claude-sonnet-5-5` for `worker: claude:sonnet` and `claude-opus-5-5` for `reviewer: claude:opus`; a `[1m]` suffix, the long-context form of the same model, counts as that model. Any other model (`claude-sonnet-5` and `claude-opus-5` among them) is a stop: the step's work goes no further (no review, no landing, no repair round), the model found is raised to Axel as an open item at once, and the agent is stopped only on his word.
- Commits: capitalised imperative subject (with a `refactor:` prefix where it fits), blank line, `- Verb ...` bullets, the last bullet the plan booking. No attribution of any kind. Never push. `merge`, `rebase`, `restore`, `push` and `reset --hard` are on the ask list.
- Design: `DESIGN.md` is the artefact every package is measured against. A disagreement between it and the code is a finding, never papered over. User-visible shapes (a config key, a wire format, the layout tree, a folder convention) are surfaced before they are baked in.
- Chrome checks go on a workbench page named TEST (Axel, 2026-09-11 23:52, "Only use the TEST page to check things in the actual website"): at every landing's Chrome check, select or create the TEST page through the page menu first and open the views there; never on Page 1 or any page Axel works on, since the layout persists every opened tab. The look on TEST may start terminals through the view's own controls, split, rename and close them, and ends every terminal it started before it finishes; it never types into, stops or closes a terminal it did not start (Axel, 2026-09-29, Z57 (a)).
- Landing procedure changes (Axel, 2026-09-13 22:45), in force from R8's landing and applied by hand until then: the package branch is brought onto main's head in the worktree before the cherry-pick (main merged into it there, the worker resolving under the checks), so no conflict is resolved on main by hand; the second review runs only after a repair round; the page check is a screenshot pass plus the package's browser spec, never a driven sequence alone. After R7 and R8, two workers may run at once on packages that touch different views (R9); the orchestrator stays one.
- Axel closes his laptop at 07:05 on 2026-09-14 (said 2026-09-13 22:05): no package is dispatched after 06:00, and at 07:05 no worker, reviewer or landing is in flight, the dispatch block reads `none`, everything is committed; the queue he agreed: D19c, R3, R7, R4, R5, R6, R8, then R9 opens two workers at a time for the features.
- Principles (Axel, 2026-09-13 22:30, binding on every remaining package and on the orchestrator's own fixes): no globals of any kind; SOLID, DRY, KISS, YAGNI, separation of concerns, high cohesion and low coupling; every config and catalog file oculus's own, not a cockpit copy. The spec's amendment of the same time states them for the workers; R3 to R7 pay the debt found on 2026-09-13.
- Conventions on every authored file: ASCII, one line per paragraph or bullet in Markdown and YAML, every source file under 1000 lines, strings in `config/text.yml`, colours as tokens, glyphs from `glyphs.yml`.
- Nothing left undone, no yes for a fix, reported means booked (Axel, 2026-09-14 00:05, after the orchestrator reported D19c's misses as gaps and asked a yes for fixing its own defects): every miss inside a brief is fixed before the package is ticked; a fix of a defect in delivered work needs no yes whatever it makes visible; what does need Axel's ruling is booked in `plan.md` or the "Open items" list above the moment it is raised and repeated in every report until he rules; every report opens with that list. Written in the `plan-orchestration` skill (step 10, Stops, Reports), the hub `CLAUDE.md` (Working rules) and `agents/spec.md` (amendment 2026-09-14 00:05).
- `plan.md` stays lean (Axel, 2026-09-13, after two rounds of bloat): a package line is one sentence, the decision, its date and the pointer to its brief, at most 400 characters; a ruling line the same; every explanation, rationale, line number, premise and landing detail lives in the brief or in this file's landing notes, never in `plan.md`. Before any commit that touches `plan.md`: `awk 'length > 400' .scratch/migration/plan.md` must print nothing but the header paragraph and the O33 and D63 package lines, whose ruling tags alone pass 400 (open item 126). And not one piece of information may be lost by shortening (Axel, 2026-09-13 22:10): when a `plan.md` line is shortened or rewritten, its previous text is first appended whole to the package's `agents/briefs/<package>.md` (a ruling's or a note's to the section "Rulings in full" below), and the commit that shortens it carries both; a line whose previous text is not in a file of this ledger is not shortened. The check: every line of the committed `plan.md` before the change is found verbatim in the ledger after it.
- Briefs and reviews from the recurring pass of 2026-10-04 (under Z251; `agents/reviews/recurring-2026-10-04.md`): every brief names, under "What is on the tree", a base copy `.agents/worktrees/<step>-base/` that the orchestrator unpacks at preparation with `git archive <base> src tests utils config ':!config/local.yml'` and beside it `unused-exports.txt`, the output of `sh utils/unused-exports.sh` on main at the base; the base copy is removed with the worktree. Every brief also lists, under "What is on the tree", the `DESIGN.md`, `HELP.md` and `README.md` paragraphs and the head comments that describe the behaviour the step changes, found by grep at /spec, and the report marks each 'still true' or 'rewritten'. Every reviewer prompt names the base copy, so a reviewer reads a file as it was there and runs no `git show`. At each review and landing the orchestrator runs `node utils/outside-paths.mjs <brief> <base>` from the worktree's root and reads its lines against the report's file table.
- Briefs and repair briefs (Axel, 2026-10-02, Z253): every repair brief says "Rewrite the report whole for the state after this round. Copy the open items from the state file at the moment you write it." in place of any "Append a section", and "After the round's last change, run the full verify list and quote its final lines in the report; a red line in a file you touched is fixed before hand-back." Every brief that introduces a key or id names it and lists its empty, duplicated and colliding cases as acceptance items. The rule sentences for builders are in `agents/spec.md`.

- Open items hold only questions with a real trade-off that needs Axel: two options that each cost something he would weigh. A choice between the correct option and an obviously wrong one, a test or a package with one sensible answer, a defect in delivered work, and work the orchestrator can do itself are decided and done, never parked as open items (Axel, 2026-10-05).
- A landing whose diff changes `package.json` or `package-lock.json` ends with `npm install --no-audit --no-fund` in main's checkout and `npm ls --depth=0` printing no `missing`, `invalid` or `UNMET` line, with the lockfile unchanged, so Axel's next start of the dev server on main has the step's dependencies; it runs only while no server of Axel's is running from main.
- No step is split: D62, D63 and D64 are one step each, and no split of any step is proposed (Axel, 2026-10-10, open item 42). A step has one repair round and never a second; a builder's work is finished, never left partial: an item of the brief the builder leaves unbuilt goes back in the one repair round, and what the round leaves is finished at landing (Axel, 2026-10-10).

- Every item of a brief names where it comes from: Axel's words, a ruling by its name, or the step's line in plan.md. An item with no such source is not written, and the brief check's prompt asks it to name every item that has none (Axel, 2026-10-13, after D71's brief added a Clone design he never asked for).
- Every builder's prompt says, as a requirement it is judged on, that it runs only the test files of what it changes while building and the verify list once at the end of each run, as `agents/spec.md` "Token discipline" says, and that its report states how many times it ran the list and any whole-suite command; a run beyond that rule is a finding of the review (Axel, 2026-10-13, after D72's builder spent 73 min on 33 whole-suite runs and waits).
- Only related tests at a landing (Axel, 2026-10-22: "From now on, during landing, you are only allowed to run tests related to files that have changed. This is now a new standing demand"): a landing runs the test files that name or import a changed file and the changed test files, wherever they are (under `tests/checks` and tagged slow included), and no other test: never `npm run test:landing`, `npm test`, `npm run test:checks`, `npm run test:slow` or the essential list of `npm run test:quick`. It replaces `npm run test:landing` wherever this file or plan.md names it for a landing (`verify_landing`, Z492). It runs as `npm run test:quick`, whose selection is the related tests only (ruling 240 (a)), in the gate worktree (ruling 241 (b), Z492).

## Open items (only what Axel must rule on; every report tells him these in plain words, decisions first, until he has ruled)

An entry leaves this list for the closed log the moment Axel rules on it. What a review or a look found and no brief covered is not an open item: it is folded into an existing step in `plan.md` (Z468), never booked as a step of its own.

- 288 (2026-10-23, /spec O55): three shapes O55 needs that no ruling names. (1) How a board and a board group name the state they take. Ruling 213 named a `type:` block and a `status:` block, each holding `words`, `not_words`, `flags` and `not_flags`, and ruling 215 keeps a group's words apart by list. Since then the manifest's type became `stage:` (ruling 252), its form moved to its own field `form:` with the list `forms:` (ruling 253), and the project chips are the list `chips:` (ruling 221); today a group has one `keys` list mixing all three (`config/defaults/layout.yml:226-300`, read by `bucketOf` in `src/lib/state/boards/board/presets.ts`), and the shipped papers and outputs groups name forms. (a) three blocks named after the vocabulary list each reads, `stages:`, `forms:` and `chips:`, as a Focus lane's `stages:` and `chips:` are named (`config/catalogs/vocab.yml:174-186`); `stages:` and `chips:` hold the four lists, `forms:` holds `words` and `not_words` only, since no form carries a flag; boards and groups take the same blocks and a group's `keys` goes. The shipped boards and groups would read: Projects `tier: research, role: lead, stages: { not_flags: [terminal] }`; Archive `tier: research, role: lead, stages: { flags: [terminal] }`; Supervision `tier: research, role: supervisor`; Coauthor `tier: research, role: [collaborator, other]`; Personal `tier: personal`; Software `tier: software`; the group in progress `stages: { words: [de-risking, exploring, in-prep, building, submit-ready, submitted, revision] }`, idea `stages: { words: [nascent, scoping] }`, papers `forms: { words: [journal, conference] }`, outputs `forms: { words: [thesis, abstract, poster, tech-report] }`, dead-ends `stages: { words: [failed, stopped] }`; the group in progress holds exactly the `live` stages, so under (a) it reads `stages: { flags: [live] }` labelled "in flight", the word ruling 275 gives the live cross-link group and the Focus board already draws (`config/text/views/boards/focus.yml:99`); its id comes from the label (`slugOf`, `state/boards/board/presets.ts:293`). Pro: one block per list, every word checked against its own list, the same names as the lanes. Con: three block names to learn. (b) the same three blocks named after the manifest field, `stage:`, `form:` and `chips:`. Pro: the name is the field a reader sees in the manifest. Con: a lane says `stages:` and a board says `stage:` for the same words. (c) the two blocks of ruling 213 only, renamed `stages:` and `chips:`, a form named through `match: { form: [journal, conference] }`, which groups would then also take. Pro: no third block. Con: a form is checked against no list, so a misspelt form is no finding, and groups gain `match:`. Recommendation: (a). (c) is the lazy option. (2) The `roles:` list of ruling 206 (2) and a project or collection entry that names no one. The fields are the manifest's: `lead:` the first author or authors, `supervisors:` the last author, `collaborators:` the other authors who count (Axel, 2026-10-24); `lead:` is in 94 of the hub's 114 manifests, `collaborators:` in 37, `supervisors:` in none. The rows, highest first: `- { key: lead, label: Lead, field: lead }`, `- { key: supervisor, label: Supervisor, field: supervisors }`, `- { key: collaborator, label: Collaborator, field: collaborators }`, `- { key: other, label: Other }`; the user's role is the first row whose field holds the person the workspace setting `self` names (`selfIds`, `state/boards/board/team.ts:18-20`), and `other` when the entry names people but not the user (21 manifests today). Naming no one (Axel 2026-10-24: is that not a finding?): 10 manifests have no `lead:` (3 software, 7 personal); no hub collection declares any of the three fields; the work collections hold their people as free text today: funding's `Role` meta row (`contributor` on ZonMw-AI-HCC, `lead - health use cases` on FAIR, `.oculus/data/academic/funding.yml:77,115`), the education entries' `Role` meta row, the events' own `role:` field. `self` is a workspace setting (`config/defaults/sources.yml:77`, set in the hub's `.oculus/workspaces/<name>.yml`) and a hub's files are shared, so one hub can be opened by several people each naming their own `self`: an entry that names no one belongs to no one in particular, and oculus cannot know that a collection is one person's (Axel 2026-10-24: what if `self` is not axel-faes?). Venues, people, organisations, radar, literature, leads, pillars, domains, data, resources and statements are registries; applications, funding, education, events and tasks hold someone's work. A collection's schema already marks a field required and the schema check raises its absence (`findings.schema.required`, `data/snapshot/schema/check.ts:226`). (a) naming no one is a finding: a project manifest without `lead:` is a finding, as one without a known `tier:` is (ruling 206 (1)), and O55's hub rewrite gives the 10 their `lead:`; a collection declares the three fields in its own `schema:` and marks `lead` required when every entry has one, the existing schema check raising a missing one; O55's hub rewrite declares them, `lead` required, on `applications.yml`, `funding.yml`, `education.yml`, `events.yml` and `tasks.yml`, writing each entry's people once (funding's and education's `Role` meta rows rewritten into the fields); a collection that declares none of the three has no roles, so its view offers no Mine only toggle, Lead badge or Role facet; there is no role for naming no one. Pro: missing people are found, not guessed, and a registry is never filtered by role. Con: the hub rewrite touches 10 manifests and every entry of five collections. (b) a project or entry that names no one takes the first role of the list (lead), and every collection takes the toggle. Pro: no rewrite. Con: a grant led by someone else that names no one shows as the user's lead, and a missing lead is never found. Recommendation: (a). (b) is the lazy option. (3) The tier flags once `projects`, `coauthor` and `archive` become `research` (Axel 2026-10-24: special behaviour attached to a tier is wrong; the `idea` flag is dropped, agreed). Every reader of a tier flag today: `main`, read by the venue load (`venueLoad`, `state/boards/ticker.ts:502`, through `state/feeds/feeds.ts:90` and `ticker.ts:159`, which counts every project with a venue whatever its stage), quick add (`liveProjects`, `state/boards/focus.ts:449`) and the tier of a new project whose form names none (`server/edit/writes/manifest.ts:615`); `software`, read by the card's Software line (`softwareTags`, `state/boards/board/items.ts:41-50`, and its label, `views/shared/card/project/CardLinks.svelte:84`) and by the board's New project button, hidden on a software board (`views/boards/projects/Projects.svelte:130`); `personal`, read by `isPersonal` (`server/content/audit/rules.ts:309`), which skips seven audit rule groups for the 17 personal projects: markers, gaps (no venue, missing field, provisional), conformance (README, folder name, code README, stage and folder), guardrails, readability (`server/content/audit/projects.ts:146,205,309,376,421`) and the publication feed (`audit/publications.ts:178,233`); `finished`, read by the Map, the venue and pillar counts, the cross-link groups and the audit (`graph.ts:97`, `venues.ts:37`, `collection.ts:787`, `projects.ts:379`); `outcome_folders` and `stage_folders`, read by the folder check (`folderStage`, `projects.ts:296-304`). (a) `tiers:` carries no flags, each tier a word and its label: `finished` goes and its readers read the stage flag `terminal`; `idea`, `outcome_folders` and `stage_folders` go, and the folder check and `audit.msg_projects_published` come back in O63 as ruling 220's hub setting (between the two landings neither finding is raised); `main` goes: the venue load counts every project with a venue at a `live` stage, quick add offers every project not at a `terminal` stage, and a new project whose form names no tier gets the first tier of the list; `software` goes: the Software line joins the Related line, which reads `related:` both ways (a project's own and those naming it), and every board offers New project, writing the board's tier; `personal` goes: the audit's research rules skip the tiers a list `research_audit: { skip_tiers: [personal] }` in `config/catalogs/vocab.yml` names. Pro: a tier is only a word a board filters on; each rule names its own scope. Con: one new key for the audit's scope. (b) as (a), with `personal: true` kept as the one tier flag. Pro: no new key. Con: one behaviour still hangs on a tier. Recommendation: (a) (Axel 2026-10-24 agreed: the audit names its own scope, `tiers:` carries no flags). The lazy option is keeping the flags `main` and `software` as they are.

- 258 (2026-10-22, O65's brief check): the step line of O65 (ruling 250) says the editor's keys work as VS Code's do and every default key Monaco binds that fails is fixed at its cause; D46 (ruling 28) says Cmd+K stays as it is, the palette's. The two collide on the keys the editor gives up to the palette (`src/lib/ui/controls/editor/chords.ts`, `freedKeys`): Cmd+K and its 31 chords (in VS Code, Cmd+K Cmd+C comments a line and Cmd+K Cmd+0 folds all, inside the editor; in oculus they run only from the palette), and on macOS Ctrl+K (in VS Code, Delete All Right in the editor; in oculus, the palette). F1, F8 and Shift+F8 are not in the clash: VS Code's workbench takes them too.
  - (a) Cmd+K and Ctrl+K stay the palette's everywhere, as D46 says; O65's key table lists the Cmd+K chords and Ctrl+K as given up to the palette under D46, each still run from the palette. Pro: nothing you press today changes. Con: the editor keeps differing from VS Code on 32 keys, which is what O65's line asks to end. This is the lazy option: it records the difference instead of ending it.
  - (b) Recommended. Inside the editor, Cmd+K is VS Code's chord key: Cmd+K then the second key runs Monaco's chord, and on macOS Ctrl+K is Delete All Right; outside the editor Cmd+K stays the palette, and in the editor the palette opens with Cmd+Shift+P, Cmd+P and F1, as in VS Code. Pro: the editor's keys are VS Code's, which O65's line asks for. Con: Cmd+K pressed in the editor no longer opens the palette; D46 changes for the editor.
  - (c) Inside the editor, Cmd+K waits for a second key: a key of one of Monaco's chords runs that chord, and any other key, or no key within the chord time, opens the palette; Ctrl+K on macOS is Delete All Right. Pro: both uses of Cmd+K stay. Con: the palette opens only after the wait or the second key, a delay on every Cmd+K in the editor, and VS Code has no such rule.
  Ruled 2026-10-22 by Axel: (b), booked in plan.md Rulings; O65's step line carries it (ruling 258).
- Open item 250 (2026-10-22, Axel's request; editor keys in general, Cmd+/ one example; fails in a Chrome tab; not locked): two parts. (1) The status bar scrolls left and right when its items do not fit. (2) The editor's keys work as VS Code's do. Run on TEST in Chrome on the fixture `tests/fixtures/hubs/demo/projects/active/streaming-anomaly/manuscript/main.tex` (each change undone, `git status` clean): Cmd+/ does nothing; typing `{` adds no `}`; Cmd+L selects nothing; Cmd+] and Alt+Down work. In `vocab.yml` Cmd+/ and Cmd+F work. Cause, read in the code: Monaco toggles a comment and closes a bracket from a language's settings (`setLanguageConfiguration`), and the editor registers its Shiki languages with an id only (`src/lib/ui/controls/editor/kit/load.ts:355-357`); `kit/rite/settings.ts` gives settings to rite, CMake, both Fortran forms, Jinja and Svelte alone, and Monaco has none of its own for `latex`, `bibtex`, `shellscript` (bash and `.slurm`), `make`, `matlab`, `ocaml`, `common-lisp` and `diff`, so in those eight Cmd+/, bracket closing, surrounding a selection and comment-aware indentation do nothing (the seven besides `latex` by reading, not run). Cmd+L is taken in a LaTeX file as the first key of the LaTeX tab's chords (`src/lib/data/helpers/keys/oculus/editor-keys.ts`, `LATEX_CHORD_FIRST`), so Monaco's select-line never runs there; Cmd+Alt+[ and ] are taken by promote and demote. The step: the eight languages get their comment, bracket, auto-closing and surrounding settings in `kit/rite/settings.ts`, with a table test that every language the editor registers has a comment rule; every default key Monaco binds is pressed in a `.tex`, a `.slurm` and a `.yml` file in Chrome and each one that fails is fixed at its cause; the LaTeX keys that take a VS Code editor key are set against what VS Code with LaTeX Workshop binds (not verified yet). (a) One new step O65 holding the status bar and the editor keys. (b) The editor keys as their own step O66, the status bar as O65. Recommended: (b), one concern each; the key part now has a found cause and a check over every key. Neither leaves work undone. Needs Axel's explicit permission for a new step. Ruled 2026-10-22 by Axel: (a), booked in plan.md Rulings; O65 added to the step list.
- Open item 251 (2026-10-22, Axel's question, redone on his reply, O53): which field names a project's people. Measured over the 113 manifests under `projects/` and `side-projects/` (a Python read of each `project.yml`): `authors:` is free text in 62 (7 of them one string, not a list), the longest list 8 names (`go-bttr`), 63 distinct strings in all, 37 of them in one manifest only; one person is spelled several ways (`Faes`, `Axel Faes`, `... Faes`; `van den Berg`, `Stephanie M. van den Berg`, `Stephanie van den Berg`), and 3 lists are cut with `...` or `et al.`; about 38 of the names are people the registry (19 people) does not hold, spread over the coauthors of the hub's papers, no single list. `collaborators:` holds person keys in 36 manifests. Oculus reads `authors:` only to draw the authors line of a project card and the Authors column of a board's list mode (`board/presets.ts:369-380`, `ProjectCard.svelte:384`, `config/defaults/layout.yml` columns); the full byline of each published paper is in the website's `_data/publications.yml` and in the manuscript. (a) A project names its people by person keys only: `lead:` (O53, a list of keys) and `collaborators:` (the registry people who work on it, the name kept); `authors:` leaves the manifest schema; the card's authors line and the Authors column draw the names of `lead:` and `collaborators:` from the registry, the lead first; the byline stays in the manuscript and on the website; the registry grows only by the people a manifest names as a key. Pro: one home per person, no free text, no long tail of one-paper coauthors in the registry. Con: a card no longer shows a paper's full byline in its order. (b) `authors:` becomes the one field, an ordered list of person keys, and `collaborators:` goes; every byline name becomes a registry person, about 38 new people. Pro: the byline order kept on the card. Con: the registry fills with coauthors the hub names nowhere else, which Axel rejected. (c) Both fields as they are: the lazy option, two places for one fact and free-text names. Recommended: (a). How the hub's 62 `authors:` values are removed is open item 254. Axel likes (a), not locked, and asked how users choose which people to add when not every author lands in `collaborators:`. Answer: under (a) nobody copies an author list; the user picks the people they work with. The Collaborators field is a picker over the people list (`config/schemas/manifest-schema.yml:57`, `type: chips, from: people`): it offers the people already in the list and cannot take a typed new name (`src/lib/views/shared/forms/entry/edit/FieldControl.svelte:135-146`, free text only for `type: tags`). A person not yet in the list is added on the People board with Add, which asks the name and opens the new person's page (`config/defaults/sources.yml:28`), then picked. A variant of (a) for Axel: (a2) as (a), and the picker also offers "Add person <typed name>" when the typed name matches nobody, which writes the new person to the people list and picks them in one step; pro: no detour to the People board; con: a new control shape on every person picker. The paper's full author list, in its printed order, stays in the manuscript and on the website. Recommended after Axel's question: (a2), since naming a new coauthor while editing a project is the common case and the detour to the People board costs every such edit; the new control is one entry at the foot of the picker's list. (c), both fields kept, is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-22 by Axel: (a2), booked in plan.md Rulings and on O53's line.
- Open item 254 (2026-10-22, from Axel's reply on 251, O53; reworded, "byline" meant a paper's author list as printed on the paper): what happens to the 62 `authors:` lines in the hub's manifests once 251 is ruled (a). - Open item 248 (2026-10-22, O61, ruling 222, `libraries: check`): the library for "a status the vocabulary does not hold is a finding naming the closest word". The closest word is the one at the smallest edit distance (Levenshtein), ties to the first in the list's order, over at most about 60 words; oculus has no such function (`grep -rniE "closest|levenshtein|editDistance" src`: none). (a) `leven` 4.1.0 (MIT, Sindre Sorhus, last release 2025-09-11, ESM, no dependencies) added as a dependency: pro, a maintained one-function package; con, one more dependency for a few lines. (b) `fastest-levenshtein` 1.0.16 (MIT, ka-weihe, last release 2022-08-02, no dependencies): pro, the fastest; con, no release in three years, and speed does not matter over 60 words. (c) written by hand in `src/lib/data/shapes/vocab-keys.ts` beside `wordFlag`: the two-row edit distance, about 15 lines, with a table test: pro, no dependency; con, a function oculus keeps. Recommended: (c), since the input is a few dozen short words and the function is small and fully tested by its table; none of the three leaves work undone, so none is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-22 by Axel: (c), booked in plan.md Rulings and on O61's line.

- Open item 213 (2026-10-22, redone): how a board selects a project by its state. A project's state is held in four kinds of thing: its type word (one of 16), the flags of that type word (11: live, terminal, dead, archive_outcome, on_map, placed, standard, idea, public, negative, dead_end), its status chips (any of 33) and the flags of those chips (17, with ruling 216's name: chip, closes_deadline, deadline_met, live_subtasks, waiting, blocked, pauses_countdown, submit_ready, published, acknowledged, lead_closed, lead_declined, lead_applied, default_lead, default_application, default_task, new_idea; of these only chip, closes_deadline, deadline_met, waiting, blocked, pauses_countdown, submit_ready, published and new_idea are carried by chip words). (a) a board names a `type:` block and a `status:` block, each holding up to four lists: `words` (the project has any of them), `not_words` (it has none of them), `flags` (one of its words carries any of these flags), `not_flags` (none of its words carries any); every list the board names must hold; a word or flag that its vocabulary does not hold is a finding; a board group (ruling 215) takes the same two blocks. Archive: `type: { flags: [terminal] }`; Projects: `type: { not_flags: [terminal] }`; in review: `status: { words: [submitted, revision] }`; blocked: `status: { flags: [blocked] }`. (b) the same eight lists as flat keys: `type_words`, `type_not_words`, `type_flags`, `type_not_flags`, `status_words`, `status_not_words`, `status_flags`, `status_not_flags`. (c) words only, the four word lists: a selection by a flag is written out as the words that carry it, and a word that gains or loses the flag is changed on every board too. (d) flags only, the four flag lists: a selection by one word needs a flag made for it. (e) (a), and a board or a group may name `any:`, a list of `type:`/`status:` pairs of which one must hold, for a selection that takes either a type word or a status chip, as today's groups do (a group's `keys` match the type or a chip): Coauthor's in progress (submitted, in-prep, exploring) and the outputs groups of Coauthor and Archive (tech-report, software as type or chip) need it to keep what they take today. Recommended: (e) if open item 218 keeps both lists, (a) if 218 (a) gives each word one list; (a) alone drops the either-or of those three groups; (b) is (a) without the grouping; (c) and (d) leave out one kind. Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.
- Open item 218 (2026-10-22): the type and the status chips both hold a project's stage. 13 words are both a type and a chip (published, submitted, in-prep, paused, tech-report, software, nogo, dropped, disproved, abandoned, exploring, phd, finished), and other stage words are chips only (submit-ready, revision, accepted, nascent, scoping, de-risking, building, running), so a project can say its stage twice and say it differently; the audit has rules for that disagreement (a published chip on unfinished work, `audit/projects.ts:401-403`; a terminal type with a live chip). (a) each word has one home: the type is the project's stage, the one word every stage word belongs to (idea, nascent, scoping, de-risking, exploring, in-prep, building, running, active, submit-ready, submitted, revision, accepted, published, paused, finished, archived, nogo, dropped, disproved, abandoned), with the flags those words carry as chips moved onto them as type flags; the chips are labels that say nothing of the stage: the form of the output (journal, conference, thesis, abstract, poster, tech-report, software, phd) and the situation (blocked, results-positive, admin-prep, numbers-pending, ai-tells); `lead` and `coauthor` go by ruling 206; the audit rules that compare the type with the chips go; a manifest that carries a stage word as a chip is a finding, and oculus's shipped vocabulary and fixtures are rewritten once. (b) both lists as now, and board groups need open item 213 (e). Recommended: (a); (b) keeps two places for one fact. Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.

- Open item 214 (2026-10-22): what in oculus's `config/` can be overridden, and where. Today a hub's `.oculus/config.yml` may replace the 24 keys of `HUB_LIST` (`data/helpers/hub-names.ts:329-354`); a workspace's `settings:` may set every other option of the five `defaults/` files; a hub's `local.yml` holds the machine keys. Nothing can change: in `vocab.yml` 15 of its 16 lists (all but `statuses`), the 7 other catalogs (`agents`, `file-nesting`, `glyphs`, `icons`, `latex`, `meta`, `unimath`), the 3 schemas (`manifest`, `deck`, `cpp`) and the interface words of `text/`. (a) each part of `config/` belongs to one place. The hub, for what the hub's records mean: every `vocab.yml` list, the new `roles:`, and added fields of the project form (`manifest-schema.yml`), a field oculus reads or writes keeping its shape. The workspace, for how one person works: the five defaults files as today, `agents`, `file-nesting`, `latex`, `unimath`, `glyphs`, `icons` and the interface words (a language). Fixed: `config/options/`, which defines every setting and checks every override, the deck and cpp schemas, which describe files oculus's own code reads, (`meta.yml` goes as a defect, step O58: no code reads it). (b) everything overridable by the hub and by the workspace alike. (c) as now. Recommended: (a); (c) is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.
